FAA Statements on Aviation Accidents and Incidents

The information below is preliminary and subject to change. News media with questions on other topics may contact us at pressoffice@faa.gov.     

For General Aviation:

•    Contact local authorities for the names and medical conditions of anyone on board.   
•    A preliminary FAA report will be posted, usually on the next business day.
•    If known, the aircraft registration number (N-number) can be searched at the Aircraft Registry. 
•    If the National Transportation Safety Board (NTSB) is also investigating, it will provide all updates. 

For Commercial Aviation:

•    Contact the airline for information about passengers and crew, flights and schedules.
•    If the National Transportation Safety Board is also investigating, it will provide all updates.

September 6, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Miami, Florida

21 Air Flight 7598 overran the runway after landing at Miami International Airport around 2 p.m. local time on Sunday, Sept. 6. The Boeing 767-300 cargo aircraft departed from Luis Muñoz Marín International Airport in San Juan, Puerto Rico. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for additional information.

General Aviation / Ludlow, Massachusetts

A Piper 28A crashed in a wooded area in Ludlow, Massachusetts around 9:15 p.m. local time on Saturday, Sept. 5. Two people were on board. The FAA and National Transportation Safety Board will investigate. The NTSB will be in charge of the investigation and will provide further updates.

Investigation and Other Information

September 3, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Baltimore, Maryland

American Airlines Flight 618 landed safely at Baltimore/Washington International Thurgood Marshall Airport around 10:15 p.m. local time on Thursday, Sept. 3, after the crew reported a passenger disturbance. The Flight departed from Dallas Fort Worth International Airport and was headed to Newark Liberty International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline and local authorities for more information.

Commercial Aviation / New York, New York

American Airlines Flight 392 aborted its takeoff roll from LaGuardia Airport in New York around 8:20 a.m. local time on Thursday, Sept. 3, after the crew reported striking a bird. The Boeing 737-800 was headed to Chicago O’Hare International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for more information.

General Aviation / Tampa, Florida

A Lancair Evolution crashed in a wooded area in Tampa, Florida, around 8:15 a.m. local time on Thursday, Sept. 3. Two people were on board. The FAA and National Transportation Safety Board will investigate. The NTSB will be in charge of the investigation and will provide further updates.

Investigation and Other Information

September 1, 2026

General Aviation / Porterville, California

A Cessna 560XL crashed while landing at Porterville Municipal Airport in California around 11:45 a.m. local time on Tuesday, Sept. 1. Seven people were on board. The FAA will investigate.

Investigation and Other Information

August 31, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Minneapolis, Minnesota

Delta Air Lines Flight 1208 returned safely to Minneapolis-Saint Paul International Airport around 11:50 a.m. local time on Monday, Aug. 31, due to a pilot medical emergency. The aircraft was headed to Orlando International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Please contact the airline for more information.

General Aviation / Hood River, Oregon

A Mooney M20P crashed near Ken Jernstedt Airfield in Oregon around 11:30 a.m. local time on Monday, Aug. 31. Two people were on board. The FAA and National Transportation Safety Board (NTSB) will investigate. The NTSB will lead the investigation and will provide further updates.

August 30, 2026

General Aviation / Enfield, New York

A Consolidated Aeronautics Lake LA-4 crashed in Enfield, New York, around 12:45 p.m. local time Sunday, Aug. 30. Two people were on board. The FAA and National Transportation Safety Board will investigate. The NTSB will lead the investigation and will provide further updates. 

August 29, 2026

General Aviation / Thermal, California

A Beechcraft Baron E55 landed in a field in Thermal, California, around 5:45 p.m. local time Saturday, Aug. 29, after the pilot reported a fuel issue. Only the pilot was on board. The FAA will investigate.

General Aviation / Portsmouth, New Hampshire

A vehicle struck a parked Pilatus PC-12 at Portsmouth International Airport in New Hampshire around 4 p.m. local time on Saturday, Aug. 29. Please contact the airport and local law enforcement for more information.

August 28, 2026

General Aviation / Las Vegas, Nevada

A Westwind 24 blew a tire and veered off the runway while it was taxiing after safely landing at Harry Reid International Airport in Las Vegas. The incident occurred around 1:45 a.m. local time on Friday, Aug. 28. Six people were on board. The FAA will investigate.

August 27, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Las Vegas, Nevada

United Airlines Flight 1403 landed safely at Harry Reid International Airport in Las Vegas around 7 a.m. local time on Thursday, Aug. 27, after the crew reported an engine issue. The Boeing 757-200 departed from Los Angeles International Airport and was traveling to Newark Liberty International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Please contact the airline for additional information. 

Investigation and Other Information

August 27, 2026

General Aviation / New Smyrna Beach, Florida

A Piper PA-18 crashed into the ocean just offshore of New Smyrna Beach, Florida, around 11:15 a.m. local time on Thursday, Aug. 27. Only the pilot was on board. The FAA will investigate.

Investigation and Other Information

August 26, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Las Vegas, Nevada

American Airlines Flight 1541 safely landed at Harry Reid International Airport in Las Vegas after the crew reported a hydraulic issue around 2:45 p.m. local time on Wednesday, Aug. 26. The Boeing 737-800 departed from Phoenix Sky Harbor International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for additional information.

August 26, 2026

Commercial Aviation / Chicago, Illinois

The crew of American Airlines Flight 3280 reported two blown tires while taxiing for departure at Chicago O'Hare International Airport around 1:35 p.m. local time on Wednesday, Aug. 26. Passengers deplaned via stairs and were bused to the terminal. The FAA will investigate. Please contact the airline for more information.

Investigation and Other Information

Republic Airways Flight 3670 was towed back to the gate at Chicago O'Hare International Airport around 2 p.m. local time Tuesday, Aug. 25, after the crew reported a brake problem while stopped on the taxiway. The Embraer 175 was headed to Detroit Metropolitan Wayne County Airport. The FAA will investigate. Please contact the airline for additional information.

A Piper PA-22 veered off the runway and crashed into a fence while taking off from Brenham Municipal Airport in Texas around 7:45 a.m. local time on Saturday, Aug. 22. Two people were on board. The FAA will investigate. 

Investigation and Other Information

General Aviation / Brenham, Texas

A Cessna 150 airplane and a Bell 407 helicopter collided in midair near Carlisle, Pennsylvania, around 7:50 p.m. local time on Wednesday, Aug. 19. Two people were on board the helicopter. The number of people on board the Cessna is unknown. The FAA and National Transportation Safety Board (NTSB) will investigate. The NTSB will lead the investigation and provide all updates.

Investigation and Other Information

Delta Air Lines Flight 2725 landed safely at Fresno Yosemite International Airport in California around 3:30 p.m. local time on Monday, July 6, after the crew reported an engine issue. Please contact the airline for additional information. 

Investigation and Other Information

July 5, 2026

Ultralight / Charles City, Iowa

An unregistered ultralight glider crashed in a field in Charles City, Iowa, around 8:45 p.m. local time on Sunday, July 5. Only the pilot was on board. 

The FAA does not investigate unregistered ultralight accidents as the vehicle does not meet the definition of an aircraft. View the FAA ultralight vehicle regulations here. The FAA defers the investigation of the accident to local law enforcement. 

General Aviation / New York, New York

The pilot of a Kodiak 100 seaplane made a hard landing in the East River near Brooklyn, New York, at about noon local time on Sunday, July 5, causing a wing strut to snap. Eight people were on board. The FAA will investigate.

Investigation and Other Information

September 28, 2024

Commercial Space / Cape Canaveral, Florida

The FAA is aware an anomaly occurred during the SpaceX NASA Crew-9 mission that launched from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station in Florida on September 28. The incident involved the Falcon 9 second stage landing outside of the designated hazard area. No public injuries or public property damage have been reported. The FAA is requiring an investigation.

Investigation and Other Information

Background

Battery transportation safety is a priority for the FAA and we provide extensive resources for passengers. The FAA recommends that passengers keep cell phones and other devices nearby in the cabin to quickly access them if necessary. Flight crews are trained to recognize and respond to lithium battery fires in the cabin. Passengers should notify the flight crew immediately if their lithium battery or device is overheating, expanding, smoking or burning.

July 11, 2024

Commercial Space / Vandenberg Space Force Base, California - UPDATED

The FAA is aware an anomaly occurred during the SpaceX Starlink Group 9-3 mission that launched from Vandenberg Space Force Base in California on July 11. The incident involved the failure of the upper stage rocket while it was in space. No public injuries or public property damage have been reported. The FAA is requiring an investigation.

Background
  
An investigation is designed to further enhance public safety, determine the root cause of the event, and identify corrective actions to avoid it from happening again. 
  
The FAA will be involved in every step of the investigation process and must approve SpaceX’s final report, including any corrective actions. 

A return to flight is based on the FAA determining that any system, process, or procedure related to the mishap does not affect public safety. In addition, SpaceX may need to request and receive approval from the FAA to modify its license that incorporates any corrective actions and meet all other licensing requirements.

jetBlue Airways Flight 369 landed safely at Raleigh-Durham International Airport around 11:20 p.m. local time on Tuesday, May 28, after the crew reported a possible hydraulic issue. Passengers deplaned on the runway and were bused to the terminal. The Airbus A321 departed from Boston Logan International Airport and was headed to Fort Lauderdale-Hollywood International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for additional information.

Commercial Aviation / Chicago, Illinois

Normal operations resumed at 2:45 p.m. local time at Chicago O’Hare International Airport after United Airlines Flight 2091 aborted takeoff due to a reported engine fire around 2 p.m. local time on Monday, May 27.  The Airbus A320 was towed to the gate where passengers safely deplaned. The FAA temporarily halted arrivals into ORD. The flight was headed to Seattle-Tacoma International Airport. Contact the airline for passenger information.

November 5, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Louisville, Kentucky

Louisville Muhammad Ali International Airport in Kentucky has reopened but multiple taxiways remain closed after UPS Flight 2976 crashed following take off around 5:15 p.m. local time on Tuesday, Nov. 4. The McDonnell Douglas MD-11 was headed to Daniel K. Inouye International Airport in Honolulu. The FAA and NTSB are on the scene investigating. The NTSB will lead the investigation and provide all updates. The FAA is coordinating with the UPS team and continue to keep them in our thoughts. You can check the status of the airport any time at fly.faa.gov.

United Airlines Flight 580 clipped the tail of United Airlines Flight 434 while taxiing on a ramp at LaGuardia Airport. Passengers on both aircraft deplaned at the gate. FAA air traffic control is not responsible for plane movements in the gate area. The FAA will investigate the incident, which occurred around 7:45 p.m. local time on Friday, Oct. 31. Contact the airline for additional information.

October 30, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Boston, Massachusetts

Air traffic control instructed Delta Air Lines Flight 263 to perform a go-around at Boston Logan International Airport because Cape Air Flight 548 was cleared to takeoff from an intersecting runway. The event occurred around 4:15 p.m. local time on Thursday, Oct. 30. The FAA is investigating.

Commercial Aviation / San Antonio, Texas

Air traffic control instructed Southwest Airlines Flight 576 to perform a go-around at San Antonio International Airport because another aircraft was slow to depart. The event occurred around 3:20 p.m. local time on Thursday, Oct. 30. The required separation was maintained.

Commercial Aviation / Tampa, Florida

JetBlue Airlines Flight 1230 diverted to Tampa International Airport around 2:00 p.m. local time on Oct. 30 after the crew experienced a flight control issue. The Airbus 320 left Cancun International Airport and was headed to Newark Liberty International Airport. The FAA is investigating. Contact the airline for more information.

Commercial Aviation / Queens, New York

Frontier Airlines Flight 3546 landed safely at John F. Kennedy Airport in New York around 12:45 p.m. local time Thursday, Oct. 30, after the crew reported an engine issue. The Airbus A320 departed from Luis Muñoz Marín International Airport in Puerto Rico. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for additional information. 

October 21, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Atlanta, Georgia

The flight crew of Delta Air Lines Flight 515 initiated a go around at Hartsfield-Jackson Atlanta International Airport, due to the previous arrival, which was slow to exit the runway. The event occurred around 2:45 p.m. local time on Tuesday, Oct. 21. Required separation was maintained.

October 20, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Omaha, Nebraska

SkyWest Flight 6469 landed safely after returning to Eppley Airfield in Omaha, Nebraska, around 7:45 p.m. local time on Monday, Oct. 20, after declaring an emergency when the pilot could not contact the cabin crew. After landing, it was determined there was a problem with the inter-phone system and the flight crew was knocking on the cockpit door. The flight was headed to Los Angeles International Airport. Contact SkyWest for more information. 

October 6, 2025

General Aviation / Sacramento, California

A Eurocopter EC130 crashed on a highway in Sacramento, California, around 7:05 p.m. local time on Monday, Oct. 6. Three people were on board. The FAA will investigate. 

October 1, 2025

Commercial Aviation / New York City, New York 

Endeavor Air Flight 5047, a CRJ-900, was taxiing inbound to its gate at LaGuardia Airport in New York when it struck Endeavor Air Flight 5155, also a CRJ-900. Air traffic control instructed flight 5155 to hold short and yield to the other aircraft.

The incident occurred at the intersection of Taxiways M and A around 10 p.m. local time on Wednesday, Oct. 1.

Please contact the airline for passenger information. The FAA is investigating.

Drone / Tolleson, Arizona

Two MK30 drones collided with the boom of a crane in Tolleson, Arizona, around 10 a.m. local time on Wednesday, Oct. 1. No injuries were reported. The FAA will investigate. 

Investigation and Other Information

Commercial Aviation / Roanoke, Virginia

Fifty-three people were on board. Passengers deplaned using portable stairs onto the EMAS bed and were bussed back to the terminal. The Embraer 145 had departed from Washington Dulles International Airport. The FAA will investigate. Please contact the airline for additional information.

Commercial Aviation / Charlotte, North Carolina

The wingtip of American Airlines Flight 2094 struck the elevator of a Cessna 750 at Charlotte Douglas International Airport in North Carolina. Flight 2094, an Airbus A319, was taxiing and the Cessna was holding on another taxiway. The FAA will investigate the incident, which occurred around 9:30 p.m. local time on Monday, Aug. 4. Please contact the airline for additional information.

Commercial Aviation / Ahmedabad, India

The FAA is in contact with the NTSB regarding Air India Flight 171, operating from Ahmedabad (AMD) to London Gatwick (LGW), that was involved in an accident in India on Thursday, June 12.

When an international incident occurs, that government leads the investigation. In the event assistance is requested, the NTSB is the official U.S. representative and the FAA provides technical support. We stand ready to launch a team immediately in coordination with the NTSB.

April 22, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Denver, Colorado

An air traffic controller instructed United Airlines Flight 2151 to perform a go-around at Denver International Airport because an aircraft was slow to exit the runway. The event occurred around 12:40 p.m. local time on Tuesday, April 22. The required separation was maintained.


Background

A go-around is a safe, routine maneuver performed at the discretion of a pilot or at the request of an air traffic controller. It discontinues the landing approach and returns the aircraft to an altitude and configuration to safely make another approach. The pilot and the air traffic controller are in full command of the situation.

The FAA has produced a video explaining the go-around process, what’s happening during the event and how pilots and air traffic controllers work together to ensure safety and efficiency as the aircraft is maneuvered for another landing approach.

Commercial Aviation / Washington, D.C.

An air traffic controller instructed American Flight 2246 to perform a go-around at Ronald Reagan Washington National (DCA) Airport to ensure separation was maintained between this aircraft and a preceding departure from the same runway. The event occurred around 8:20 a.m. local time on Tuesday, February 25, 2025. 

Background
A go-around is a safe, routine maneuver performed at the discretion of a pilot or at the request of an air traffic controller. It discontinues the landing approach and returns the aircraft to an altitude and configuration to safely make another approach. The pilot and the air traffic controller are in full command of the situation.

The crew of United Airlines Flight 1382 safely aborted its takeoff from the George Bush Intercontinental/Houston Airport in Texas due to a reported engine issue around 8:35 a.m. local time on Sunday, Feb. 2. Passengers deplaned on the runway and were bused to the terminal. The Airbus A319 was flying to LaGuardia Airport in New York. The FAA will investigate. Contact the airline for additional information.

Local authorities will provide the names of the people involved. The FAA investigates the packing of the main and reserve parachutes, and the rules of flight for the pilot and aircraft. If the FAA does not find any evidence of regulatory violation, it will defer any further investigation of the accident to local law enforcement. The FAA does not determine cause.

January 28, 2025

Commercial Aviation / Washington, D.C.

The crew of Republic Airways Flight 4514 initiated a go-around at Ronald Reagan Washington National Airport (DCA) in response to an onboard alert that a military helicopter was nearby. Air traffic controllers issued traffic advisories to both aircraft and the military helicopter crew reported they had the Republic aircraft in sight.  There was no loss of required separation between the aircraft.
 
The event occurred around 8 p.m. local time on Tuesday, Jan. 28.
